## Document 021: 20261005_投資週報_製造業加速擴張，AI需求推動市場

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261005_投資週報_製造業加速擴張，AI需求推動市場.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.50-159.00

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1400 
前週交易區間 
1.1214 1.1391

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.29-115.10.02 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週為提存期底 ， 繼央行上週大砍存單效應持續發酵 ， 市
場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

---

3Q26 營收低於預期，營運逐季向上方向不變 
 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%， 低於預期 ，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元:雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 
10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。2Q26 營收
87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較 1Q26
衰退 1.2 個百分點，亦低於預估的 30.5%，主要是報廢品打消，預期
3Q26 毛利率回復至 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。3Q26 營收
97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，低於
預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，實際3Q26 拉貨力道未如預期顯
著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。

冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量 ，TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch 
tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出貨 ，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU
客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元：2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因
素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢明確，故維持買進，預

雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
 雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月 ， 為國內前二大NB 散熱模組廠 ， 過去主要生產散熱器 、
散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解決
方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。2Q26 產品應用領域 Server 78%、PC 17%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外水冷業務比重於 1H26 約為 55~60%。

2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，
2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較
1Q26 衰退 1.2 個百分點 ， 亦低於預估的30.5%， 主要是報廢品打消 ， 預期3Q26
毛利率回復上 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。

雙鴻 3Q26 營收 97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，
低於預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，故推估基期過高，實際 3Q26 拉貨力
道未如預期顯著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號         Page 3 of 5 
個股報告

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊
業務均樂觀

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務均樂觀 
2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。
氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～
7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以 AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%
對比 20~30%，TR4 將有機會為 100%全水冷 ， 水冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量，
TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出
貨，Compute tray 水冷產值 3000usd 以上，Switch tray 水冷產值 1000usd 以
上，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU 客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。

1H26 GB 水冷營收中 Rack manifolds 佔比 30-35%、Cold Plate+inner manifolds
佔比 55-60%、CDU 5% ，2026 年可視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。
公司展望 3Q26 營收+15~20%QOQ，實際+12.5%QOQ 低於預期，4Q26 營收
QOQ+雙位數，2026 年營收+70~85%YOY，3Q26 毛利率將回到 29.7%以上水
準，2027 年維持正向看法。

維持買進，目標價 1800 元

維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元 
2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢
明確，故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 55.74 元，預估 2027 年 EPS 90.36 元，
目標價 1800 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

 상대 (3.2) 1.6  63.7   
 절대 (달러환산) 0.3  (11.5) 239.5

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

## Document 221: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다. 
목표주가 (하향) 2,000,000원  
현재가 (10/6) 1,310,000원

## Document 303: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

### Page 2

  I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).  
Exhibit 6: For restaurants and bars the story is mixed across older

of all households, although it remains below its 2019 level (Exhibit 7).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

older cohorts

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

貨幣監理署 (OCC)發布公告敲 定GENIUS法
案最終實施細則 ，疊加近期機構投資者對加
密貨幣接受度 提升推動近期 漲勢。匯市主要
受到美伊戰事添通膨變數 ，投資人靜 待下週
聯準會利率決策結果，美元相對承壓。

► 今年以來適 逢美伊衝突難題遲遲未解 ，高油
價讓聯準會重啟升息且堆高升息 預期，面對
部分聯準會官員保持觀望立場 ，利率期貨顯
示聯準會將升息一碼 且時點可能在 12月，未
來1年則存在3碼潛在升息空間 ，考量地緣衝
突短期 內持 續 ， 若 通膨難 以改善 至政 策目
標，預期聯準會2027年將維持緊縮貨幣政策
的鷹派立場來對抗通膨，然美國2年期公債利
率已多反映 未來升息預期 ，後續仍視美國核
心物價發展，引導短天期公債利率走向。
► 儘管全球利率大幅上升 ，然對於整體 金融環
境影響 甚微，反映美 股和信用 市場低波動狀
態，體現實質 政策利率低於 緊縮利率 區間與
彭博金 融情勢指數變化不大 ，當前美國貨幣
政策與市場流動性仍偏寬鬆 ，倘若地緣擾動

持續造成通 膨居高難下 ，恐進一步推升聯準
會升息預期 ，疊加政府財政 風險與貨幣政策
透明度降 ，加劇長 天期公債利率 上行風險 ，
長天期債以交易為主，不建議長期持有。

► 特斯拉3Q26財報好壞參半 — 毛利率與交付
量超預 期 ， 營收 大 致符合 ，營業 利潤 遜預
期，最大意外是自由現金流達 15億美元，高
於共識負43億，資本開支指引下調成憂慮。
► 早前公司展示下代 Roadster能力，本質上是
光環車 ，定價高受眾少 ，2028 年才有望投
產，盈利貢獻有限 。Model 3/Y成為大眾商
品，競爭激烈 ，利潤率壓縮 。正如傳統品牌
以賽道或參賽展示技術 ，「飛天」旨在展示
獨有跨界整合能力，維持品牌形象溢價。
► 至於和SpaceX的潛在合併，核心在於貫通多
頂 業 務， 提 高 資 本 運 用 效 率， 共同分擔
Terafab 的開支等 ，現時兩家合計市值約為
3.5兆美元。這項巨型潛在交易 ，除了要跨過
極高表決門檻外，SpaceX具極敏感的美國國
防及NASA合作背景 ，而特斯拉有超過 20%
業務倚重上海超級工廠 ，合併存在嚴重地緣
衝突。預期馬斯克將在川普離任前 ，加速執
行Tesla與SpaceX 合併。

► 今年9月適逢地緣擾動持續推升高油價、聯準
會重啟 升息 、財政部 與企 業發債 浪潮 等 影
響，形塑債市今年以來單月最差表現 。檢視
2021年以來美國投資級債前十大單月最差總
報酬，2022年聯準會 3月啟動升息年度就佔
一半以上，顯示利率風險仍為近5年來債市最
大風險 ，然檢視單月最差報酬表現未來期間
總報酬，除2022年1月和3月聯準會重啟升息
前夕與當月表現明顯較弱，多數於未來1年後
多呈現正報酬 ，反映隨利率走升放大孳息效
益，同時挹注債券長期持有之報酬空間。
► 今年來債券市場利率波動明顯推升信用債利
差，其 中僅 以低 信 評債利 差升幅 較顯 ，此
外，9月以來利率連動度高及AI相關產業債種
也成為利差擴大重災區 ，凸顯債市投資人減
輕市場不確定性的投資傾向 ，考量信用債市
歷經利率上行利空洗禮 ，信用利差同步擴大

創造收益空間 ，可把握債信體質仍佳企業債
鎖利良機，並透過時間複利力量強化收益。
利率波動升，僅低信評利差擴大程度相對明顯9月為美國投資級債今年最差單月報酬
用時間換空間，單月最差表現形塑未來報酬機會

### Page 8

8
► 受聯準會重啟升息影響 ，過去一年美國公債
殖利率曲線持續陡峭化 ，其中利率最大升幅
落在短天期的 2至3年期間，美國核心通膨與
就業仍具韌性 ，聯準會將持續保持 緊縮貨幣
政策步調來應對高通膨 ，考量美國高物價與
高利率環境短期 難消，宜透過縮短債券配置
天期並進行動態調整 ，為因應後續利率不確
定性環境的良策。
► 為因應利率不確定性 ，投資人可採取債券梯
的投資配置 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行 組合，債券梯投資重點主要在
於，根據殖利率曲線型態及利率動態建立不
同期間 債券梯，假設市場利率走升且殖利率
曲線正斜率 ，建立短天期債券梯除了降低利
率風險 ，有 短期 閒 置資金 及孳息 需求 投資
人，亦可取得穩定孳息 收益。若後續投資人
有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將短期債券到
期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至
到期或直接變現的流動性風險。
投資焦點
美國公債殖利率曲線持續為正斜率
高利率波動持續，用策略迎戰市場擾動
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理

建立短天期債券梯，分散風險及穩定現金流
債券梯
經過第2年第一檔 債券到期，視資金需求 及市況與利率 動
態決定後續配置

短天期債券梯 ：假設買入 2檔各 3、4、5及7年到期債券 ，
聚焦 短期穩定收益
► 債券梯是一種分散投資方式 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，可兼具：
1) 分散利率風險： 將資金分別買進不同到期年期的債券 ，以分
散利率風險。
2) 分散信用風險： 分散投資不同發行 機構債券，不會重壓 單一
債券並可分散信用違約風險。
3) 降低流動性風險： 若投資人有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將
短期債券到期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至到期
或直接變現之流動性風險。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境 使中短天期債券提供
吸引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後
利差具吸引力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

### Page 18

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

## Document 372: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-27).pdf

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.
Q4 2026 forecasts:
Brent crude
US$101/bbl
Japanese LNG
US$24.5/MMBtu

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

### Page 3

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

### Page 4

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-
Quarterly forecast inflation profile

Source: ABS, Westpac Economics.
pate. Another rate hike in November, along with a deeper than 
expected fall in established home prices, could be the prover -
bial “straw” resulting in a larger than expected fall in demand 
and thus a more significant moderation in price setting behav -
iour. It is also possible that our auto fuel prices fall by more 
than we anticipate. For while we have spot crude prices to fall 
to about pre-crisis levels by end 2027, a combination of a slow 
recovery in refining capacity, constrained return of physical 
supplies and higher shipping cost keep Australian fuel prices 
above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

## Document 390: SK海力士三季度营业利润同比暴增7倍，祭出百万亿韩元史诗级股东回报，存储芯片供应短缺持续至2030年

Source PDF: all report pdf/SK海力士三季度营业利润同比暴增7倍，祭出百万亿韩元史诗级股东回报，存储芯片供应短缺持续至2030年.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 海⼒⼠三季度营收⾸破 100 万亿韩元，传统 DRAM 和 NAND ⻬涨 20% 成盈利主引擎，但
净利润意外不及预期。其巨额分红特含铠侠投资收益。公司已锁定 14 家⼤客⼾未来两年超
半数产能，同时警告持续涨价或将反噬 PC 与⼿机终端需求。
摘要：
SK 海⼒⼠交出了⼀份喜忧参半的三季度成绩单：营业收⼊历史上⾸次突破 100 万亿韩元，营业
收⼊和营业利润环⽐分别增⻓ 32% 和 38% ，净利润却意外不及预期。
这家全球第⼆⼤内存芯⽚制造商周三（ 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇）公布，第三季度营业利润同⽐飙升七倍⾄
83.33 万亿韩元，远超市场预期的 76.27 万亿韩元，超出幅度约 9% ；营收亦达 104.62 万亿韩元，
较市场预期的 97.34 万亿韩元⾼出约 7% 。然⽽，净利润却意外爆冷 —— 仅录得 39.53 万亿韩元，
远低于市场预期的 60.73 万亿韩元，成为本季财报最⼤的意外变量。
与此同时， SK 海⼒⼠抛出⼀份⼒度空前的股东回报计划，宣布 2026 财年将合计向股东返还约

100 万亿韩元（约合 750 亿美元），涵盖逾 30 万亿韩元现⾦分红及逾 60 万亿韩元股份回购与注
销。其中，第三季度每股派息 20,000 韩元，包含源⾃对⽇本铠侠（ Kioxia ）投资收益的每股
10,000 韩元特别股息。公司还新批 20 万亿韩元回购计划，约半数⽤于股东回报后将予以注销。
在前景判断上， SK 海⼒⼠态度颇为乐观。公司表⽰，存储芯⽚供应短缺将持续⾄ 2030 年，季度
盈利尚未触及峰值，⾄少在 2027 年前上升势头不会逆转。公司同时预计， 2027 年 DRAM 市场需
求将同⽐增⻓ 20% 中段， NAND 需求增速则在⾼⼗位数百分⽐区间。
SK 海⼒⼠还表⽰，随着⻰仁⼀期晶圆⼚明年投产，以及国内外基础设施投资推进，公司明年投
资规模预计将较今年⼤幅增加。公司计划根据客⼾需求、产品竞争⼒、盈利能⼒及投资效率，
灵活调整投资规模和时机，并预计明年营收及营业利润将提升⾄新的规模⽔平。
财报公布之后， SK 海⼒⼠盘前在 Nextrade 平台的报价显⽰涨幅约 3% 。尽管本季财报数据扎实，

但这⼀涨幅与业绩表现相⽐显得较为温和。
分析认为，此番反应折射出近期 AI 产业链财报季的普遍规律 —— 业绩超预期往往难以重燃市场此
前的亢奋情绪。与此同时，美联储利率决议及 Alphabet 、微软财报将于同⽇公布，令投资者在
⼤举押注前保持审慎。
营收与营业利润：双双碾压预期， AI 需求持续催化
SK 海⼒⼠第三季度营收 104.62 万亿韩元和营业利润 83.33 万亿韩元的组合，是对外界有关 AI 基础
设施投资能否持续所产⽣质疑的有⼒回应。
⽀撑这份强劲业绩的核⼼逻辑，来⾃ DRAM 和 NAND 价格的双轮驱动。公司披露，第三季度
DRAM 均 价 环 ⽐ 上 涨 ⾼ 达 20% 区 间 ， NAND 均 价 环 ⽐ 上 涨 20% 中 段 区 间 。 Counterpoint
Research 研究总监 Hwang MS 指出， “HBM 固然在经历价格上涨，但本季度盈利的⼤幅跃升，主
要归因于传统 DRAM 和 NAND 的快速提价。 ”
从⼤背景看， AI 数据中⼼的内存⽀出正在持续扩张。⻨格理研究数据显⽰，内存⽬前已占 AI 数据

中⼼总费⽤的约三分之⼀，且这⼀⽐例预计将随 HBM 价格在 2027 年翻倍以上⽽进⼀步攀升。SK
海⼒⼠作为英伟达最重要的 HBM 供应商，正是这波 AI 基础设施军备竞赛的核⼼受益⽅。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

净利润：落差 35% 背后有何隐情
尽管营业层⾯全⾯超预期，净利润 39.53 万亿韩元却较预期的 60.73 万亿韩元低了约 21 万亿韩
元，落差幅度⾼达 35% ，是本季财报最令市场意外之处。
从财务结构推断，营业利润与净利润之间的巨⼤剪⼑差，可能源于多重因素叠加：包括铠侠投
资相关的税务处理、汇率损益、⼀次性⾮经营性项⽬，以及公司⼤规模股东回报计划在会计层
⾯的影响。
这⼀落差提醒投资者，在解读 SK 海⼒⼠的盈利质量时，需要将营业利润与净利润分开审视。公
司运营层⾯的盈利能⼒依然强劲，但净利润层⾯的⼤幅不及预期，或在短期内对市场情绪形成
⼀定扰动。
股东回报：百万亿韩元史诗级计划全⾯解读
SK 海⼒⼠此次股东回报计划的规模，堪称公司历史上的⾥程碑。
现⾦分红⽅⾯，全年现⾦股息总额将超过 30 万亿韩元。第三季度每股 20,000 韩元的股息中，有
10,000 韩元反映季度经营业绩，另外 10,000 韩元则来⾃铠侠投资收益的特别分红。公司表⽰，
将通过特别股息的形式，把铠侠投资所获的⼤部分收益返还给股东，分两期⽀付 —— 分别纳⼊

第三季度股息及 2026 年年终股息。
股份回购⽅⾯，公司计划回购并注销逾 60 万亿韩元的股份。今⽇新批的 20 万亿韩元回购计划
中，约 10 万亿韩元将⽤于股东回报后注销，另外 10 万亿韩元分配给员⼯薪酬。未来每季度将由
董事会根据市况、现⾦流等因素决定后续回购规模。
整体⽽⾔，此次百万亿韩元的回报承诺，既是对此前因股东反对和监管审查⽽搁置 Solidigm 赴美
上市计划的⼀次补偿性安抚，也是公司对⾃⾝⻓期盈利能⼒充满信⼼的主动宣⽰。
存储芯⽚短缺持续⾄ 2030 年，峰值盈利仍在远⽅
SK 海⼒⼠对⾏业未来的判断相当确定 —— 存储芯⽚供应短缺将贯穿整个 2020 年代后半段，直⾄
2030 年。
具体到需求预测，公司预计 2027 年 DRAM 市场需求将同⽐增⻓ 20% 中段区间， NAND 市场需求
则增⻓⾼⼗位数百分⽐，显⽰两⼤核⼼产品线均处于强劲需求周期。
对于存储市场， SK 海⼒⼠展望短期由企业级和消费级 AI 引领需求，中⻓期则由⾃动驾驶、机器
⼈等物理 AI 驱动，市场将延续增⻓态势。

另⼀⽅⾯， SK 海⼒⼠认为，供给端未能跟上市场增⻓速度。由于先进制程技术难度不断提升，
且新建⽣产设施耗时较⻓，预计到 2030 年为⽌，供不应求的格局将持续。
SK 海 ⼒ ⼠ 表 ⽰ ， 已 与 包 括 全 球 ⼤ 型 云 服 务 提 供 商 在 内 的 1 4 家 主 要 客 ⼾ 签 署 ⻓ 期 供 应 协 议
（ LTA ），并正与部分客⼾提前磋商延⻓协议期限，现有协议期限通常为 5 年。公司称，截⾄⽬前
已锁定的 LTA 供应量相当于 2027 年和 2028 年总产能的 50% 以上。
不过，公司也指出了⼀个隐忧：随着内存价格上涨和供应短缺加剧， PC 和智能⼿机出货量预计
将出现下滑。这意味着消费电⼦端需求存在⼀定压制，终端市场的价格弹性边界正在被测试。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s advance GDP 
estimate for 3Q26 to register above -
trend growth of 5.0% yoy, albeit 
moderating from 6.0% yoy  in 2Q26. The 
overall expansion continued to be 
supported by ongoing strength in the 
export-oriented manufacturing sector, 
underpinned by strong  AI-related 
demand for electronics, and sustained 
domestic demand, backed by healthy 
investment and private consumption 
trends. However, contraction in mining 
and weak agricultural activity dragged 
overall growth.

Singapore

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

China

Exports growth is expected to remain 
robust at 25.7% y oy in September, 
supported by continued strength in 
electronics demand. Both the official and 
Caixin manufacturing PMIs rose above 
the 50 expansion threshold during the 
month, pointing to improving 
manufacturing activity. External trade 
indicators also stren gthened, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage 
handled at China's 20 major ports 
accelerating from -0.7% yoy in August to 
7.2% in September. Freight activity 
improved as well, as growth in 
international cargo flights picked up 
from -1.4% y oy to 3.6% over the same 
period.

On the price front, factory -gate and raw 
material prices rebounded amid the 
renewed escalation in geopolitical 
tensions. Main raw material purchasing 
price and producer price subPMI 
increased from 56.6 and 50.4 to 60.8 and 
54.0 during the period. However, 
downstream consumer price pressures 
are likely to remain relatively subdued 
under the pricing-band mechanism. As a 
result, CPI inflation is expected to edge 
up from 0.8% y oy in August to 1.0% in 
September. Nevertheless, soft domestic 
demand is expected to continue 
constraining the pace of consumer price 
increases.

India

September inflation is likely to gather 
momentum to 5.7% y/y, partly driven by 
base effects, from 4.8% the month 
before. Food segments continue to 
register gains, with pressures 
broadening to include perishables, 
edible oils, rice, pulses, sugar, etc., 
according to high-frequency data. Under 
non-food, retail pump prices have not 
been adjusted further, though other fuel 
products, such as non -subsidised LPG, 
diesel and ATF (still up by double digits 
y/y, though slower than in August), are 
up on the year. Core  inflation is also 
likely to tick up, aligning with the central 
bank's view that price risks are no longer 
benign. Pressures have built up due to 
the overhang of an uneven and sub -par 
monsoon, drought conditions in parts of 
the country, and elevated oil pr ices. In 
the face of higher headline prints, we 
expect the RBI to keep the door open for 
another rate hike in December 2026.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable,

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.
